id="monocular-depth-estimation-with-ultralytics-yolo" class="group relative">Monocular Depth Estimation with Ultralytics YOLO<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#monocular-depth-estimation-with-ultralytics-yolo">#</a></h1>
<img alt="Monocular depth estimation examples" decoding="async" loading="lazy" src="https://cdn.ul.run/i/7a53b502cdffcaa1bbf61ca1690a2dde.avif" width="1024"/>
<p>Monocular depth estimation predicts a per-pixel depth map from a single RGB image. Each output pixel holds a depth value in meters representing the estimated distance from the camera to that surface point.</p>
<p>The output of a depth model is a dense float map of shape <code>(H, W)</code> aligned to the input image. This per-pixel representation makes monocular depth estimation well-suited for 3D scene reconstruction, robot navigation, AR/VR content creation, and any application that requires spatial layout from a single camera.</p><section class="admon admon-success"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-lightbulb admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M15 14c.2-1 .7-1.7 1.5-2.5 1-.9 1.5-2.2 1.5-3.5A6 6 0 0 0 6 8c0 1 .2 2.2 1.5 3.5.7.7 1.3 1.5 1.5 2.5"></path><path d="M9 18h6"></path><path d="M10 22h4"></path></svg><span>Tip</span></div><div class="admon-content"><p>Use <code>task=depth</code> or the <code>yolo depth</code> CLI task for monocular depth estimation. YOLO26 depth model files use the <code>-depth</code> suffix, such as <code>yolo26n-depth.pt</code>.</p></div></section><div align="center">

<h2 id="models" class="group relative"><a href="https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/tree/main/ultralytics/cfg/models/26" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Models</a><a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#models">#</a></h2>
<p>YOLO26 depth models pretrained on a broad multi-dataset mix (indoor + outdoor, ~2.19M images) are shown below. The metrics columns are reported on the <a href="https://cs.nyu.edu/~fergus/datasets/nyu_depth_v2.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">NYU Depth V2</a> Eigen test split.</p>
<p><a href="https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/tree/main/ultralytics/cfg/models" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Models</a> download automatically from the latest Ultralytics <a href="https://github.com/ultralytics/assets/releases" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">release</a> on first use.</p>







































































<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Model</th><th>size<br/><sup>(pixels)</sup></th><th>delta1<sup>NYU</sup></th><th>abs_rel<sup>NYU</sup></th><th>rmse<sup>NYU</sup></th><th>Speed<br/><sup>CPU ONNX<br/>(ms)</sup></th><th>Speed<br/><sup>T4 TensorRT10<br/>(ms)</sup></th><th>params<br/><sup>(M)</sup></th><th>FLOPs<br/><sup>(B)</sup></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><a href="https://platform.ultralytics.com/ultralytics/yolo26/yolo26n-depth" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26n-depth</a></td><td>768</td><td>0.882</td><td>0.109</td><td>0.414</td><td><strong>272.0 ± 27.2</strong></td><td><strong>2.7 ± 0.1</strong></td><td><strong>6.3</strong></td><td><strong>46.9</strong></td></tr><tr><td><a href="https://platform.ultralytics.com/ultralytics/yolo26/yolo26s-depth" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26s-depth</a></td><td>768</td><td>0.896</td><td>0.104</td><td>0.399</td><td>393.7 ± 13.1</td><td>3.8 ± 0.0</td><td>13.2</td><td>68.0</td></tr><tr><td><a href="https://platform.ultralytics.com/ultralytics/yolo26/yolo26m-depth" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26m-depth</a></td><td>768</td><td>0.921</td><td>0.089</td><td>0.364</td><td>621.5 ± 49.7</td><td>6.0 ± 0.1</td><td>23.3</td><td>130.4</td></tr><tr><td><a href="https://platform.ultralytics.com/ultralytics/yolo26/yolo26l-depth" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26l-depth</a></td><td>768</td><td>0.930</td><td>0.083</td><td>0.351</td><td>821.9 ± 50.7</td><td>7.7 ± 0.1</td><td>27.7</td><td>157.0</td></tr><tr><td><a href="https://platform.ultralytics.com/ultralytics/yolo26/yolo26x-depth" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26x-depth</a></td><td>768</td><td><strong>0.933</strong></td><td><strong>0.080</strong></td><td><strong>0.344</strong></td><td>1240.9 ± 73.3</td><td>13.6 ± 0.2</td><td>57.0</td><td>301.7</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<ul>
<li><strong>delta1<sup>NYU</sup></strong> is the percentage of pixels where the predicted depth is within a factor of 1.25 of the ground truth, on the NYU Depth V2 Eigen test split (654 images) with multi-scale + horizontal-flip TTA and log-least-squares alignment.</li>
<li>Single-scale accuracy without TTA is reproducible with <code>yolo depth val model=yolo26n-depth.pt data=nyu-depth.yaml imgsz=768 device=0</code> (substitute <code>model=</code> for each size), which uses median (scale-only) alignment and scores lower: delta1 0.783 (n), 0.793 (s), 0.840 (m), 0.853 (l), 0.860 (x).</li>
<li><strong>abs_rel</strong> is the mean absolute relative error between predicted and ground-truth depth values.</li>
<li><strong>rmse</strong> is the root mean squared error in meters.</li>
<li><strong>Speed</strong> is inference-only latency (pre/post-processing excluded) at <code>imgsz=768</code>, <code>batch=1</code>, reported as mean ± std over timed runs after warmup. <strong>CPU ONNX</strong> is ONNX Runtime fp32 on a 32-core Intel Xeon (Skylake); <strong>T4 TensorRT10</strong> is TensorRT fp16 on a Tesla T4.</li>
<li><strong>params</strong> and <strong>FLOPs</strong> are measured at 768×768, the training resolution of the released weights.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="speed-compared-to-depth-anything-v2" class="group relative">Speed compared to Depth Anything V2<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#speed-compared-to-depth-anything-v2">#</a></h2>
<p>Depth Anything V2 is a widely used open baseline for monocular depth. Its DINOv2 <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/vision-transformer-vit" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">vision transformer</a> backbone and DPT decoder are compute-heavy, so on the same Tesla T4 under TensorRT fp16 the smallest released Depth Anything V2 model is slower than every YOLO26 depth model — including YOLO26x-depth, which carries more than twice the parameters.</p>
<p>At ~768 px — <code>imgsz=768</code> for YOLO26, the resolution its released weights are trained at, and 770 px for Depth Anything V2, whose DINOv2 backbone requires a multiple of its patch size of 14:</p>













































































<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Model</th><th style="text-align:right">params (M)</th><th style="text-align:right">FLOPs (B)</th><th style="text-align:right">T4 TensorRT10 (ms)</th><th style="text-align:right">FPS</th><th style="text-align:right">Speedup vs V2 Small</th><th style="text-align:right">Speedup vs V2 Base</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Depth Anything V2 Base</td><td style="text-align:right">97.5</td><td style="text-align:right">1025.5</td><td style="text-align:right">55.40</td><td style="text-align:right">18.1</td><td style="text-align:right">—</td><td style="text-align:right">—</td></tr><tr><td>Depth Anything V2 Small</td><td style="text-align:right">24.8</td><td style="text-align:right">346.9</td><td style="text-align:right">20.99</td><td style="text-align:right">47.6</td><td style="text-align:right">—</td><td style="text-align:right">—</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26x-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">57.0</td><td style="text-align:right">301.7</td><td style="text-align:right">13.57</td><td style="text-align:right">73.7</td><td style="text-align:right">1.5×</td><td style="text-align:right">4.1×</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26l-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">27.7</td><td style="text-align:right">157.0</td><td style="text-align:right">7.68</td><td style="text-align:right">130.2</td><td style="text-align:right">2.7×</td><td style="text-align:right">7.2×</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26m-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">23.3</td><td style="text-align:right">130.4</td><td style="text-align:right">6.00</td><td style="text-align:right">166.7</td><td style="text-align:right">3.5×</td><td style="text-align:right">9.2×</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26s-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">13.2</td><td style="text-align:right">68.0</td><td style="text-align:right">3.82</td><td style="text-align:right">261.6</td><td style="text-align:right">5.5×</td><td style="text-align:right">14.5×</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26n-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">6.4</td><td style="text-align:right">46.9</td><td style="text-align:right">2.73</td><td style="text-align:right">365.8</td><td style="text-align:right">7.7×</td><td style="text-align:right">20.3×</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>At ~640 px — <code>imgsz=640</code> and 644 px respectively — the ordering is unchanged, and YOLO26n-depth is 5.9× faster than Depth Anything V2 Small:</p>













































<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Model</th><th style="text-align:right">T4 TensorRT10 (ms)</th><th style="text-align:right">FPS</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Depth Anything V2 Base</td><td style="text-align:right">35.10</td><td style="text-align:right">28.5</td></tr><tr><td>Depth Anything V2 Small</td><td style="text-align:right">13.62</td><td style="text-align:right">73.4</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26x-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">10.26</td><td style="text-align:right">97.5</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26l-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">6.14</td><td style="text-align:right">162.8</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26m-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">4.71</td><td style="text-align:right">212.1</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26s-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">3.08</td><td style="text-align:right">324.4</td></tr><tr><td>YOLO26n-depth</td><td style="text-align:right">2.29</td><td style="text-align:right">436.9</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<ul>
<li>Latency is inference-only, <code>batch=1</code>, TensorRT fp16 on a Tesla T4 — the same harness as the model table above, shown here to two decimals; FPS is the reciprocal of the unrounded latency. The <strong>Speedup</strong> columns divide the Depth Anything V2 Small (20.99 ms) and Base (55.40 ms) latency by the YOLO26 latency.</li>
<li>Depth Anything V2 Small and Base are the released ViT-S and ViT-B checkpoints.</li>
<li>The comparison covers latency only — the two model families are not evaluated here under a shared accuracy protocol.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="depth-range-and-the-log-depth-head" class="group relative">Depth range and the log-depth head<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#depth-range-and-the-log-depth-head">#</a></h2>
<p>The depth head predicts <code>exp(logit)</code> — <strong>unbounded</strong> (~0.02–150 m) — and <strong>decouples scene shape from absolute scale</strong>: the network predicts a relative log-depth field, and absolute meters are set by a separate two-parameter transform (<code>exp(a·log d + b)</code>) recovered at evaluation, by lightweight calibration, or by fine-tuning. The common alternative, a bounded <code>sigmoid × max_depth</code> head, instead bakes a fixed ceiling into the architecture, so any depth beyond <code>max_depth</code> is clipped — which prevents training on, and predicting, longer-range scenes.</p>
<h3 id="why-the-head-is-unbounded-evidence-across-depth-ranges" class="group relative">Why the head is unbounded: evidence across depth ranges<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#why-the-head-is-unbounded-evidence-across-depth-ranges">#</a></h3>
<p><strong>Controlled A/B — same data, same schedule, only the head differs.</strong> Training both heads from scratch on an identical mix of indoor (≤10 m) and outdoor (≤80 m) data:</p>























<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Head</th><th>Output range</th><th style="text-align:right">Mixed-range val δ1</th><th>Training behavior</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>sigmoid × max_depth</code> (10 m)</td><td>bounded 0–10 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.221</td><td><strong>plateaus at epoch 1</strong></td></tr><tr><td><code>log</code> (unbounded)</td><td>0–~150 m</td><td style="text-align:right"><strong>0.367</strong> (+66%)</td><td>improves throughout</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>The bounded head cannot represent the &gt;10 m majority of outdoor pixels, so it stops improving almost immediately; the <code>log</code> head learns from the full range.</p>
<p><strong>The failure mode it fixes — single-dataset fine-tuning, stratified by range.</strong> Fine-tuning a bounded (10 m) head on individual datasets works when the data fits the cap and collapses when it exceeds it:</p>






























<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Dataset</th><th>Depth range</th><th style="text-align:right">Bounded-head δ1</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>SUN RGB-D</td><td>≤10 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.78 ✅</td></tr><tr><td>Hypersim</td><td>mostly ≤10 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.74 ✅</td></tr><tr><td>KITTI</td><td>~80 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.08 ❌ (abs_rel ≈ 7.0 — the 80/10 scale mismatch)</td></tr><tr><td>vKITTI2</td><td>80 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.04 ❌</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>The collapse lands exactly at the cap boundary. The <code>log</code> head has no such boundary.</p>
<p><strong>Released models — cross-range performance.</strong> The shipped <code>log</code>-head family, evaluated across benchmarks spanning 10 m (NYU, iBims-1) to 80 m (KITTI), with scale-aligned δ1:</p>















































<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Benchmark</th><th>Range</th><th style="text-align:right">YOLO26x-depth (<code>log</code>)</th><th style="text-align:right">prior bounded release</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>NYU Eigen</td><td>10 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.933</td><td style="text-align:right">0.934</td></tr><tr><td>iBims-1</td><td>10 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.961</td><td style="text-align:right">0.945</td></tr><tr><td>ETH3D</td><td>~60 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.959</td><td style="text-align:right">0.931</td></tr><tr><td>Make3D</td><td>~70 m</td><td style="text-align:right">0.302</td><td style="text-align:right">0.296</td></tr><tr><td>KITTI Eigen</td><td>80 m</td><td style="text-align:right"><strong>0.942</strong></td><td style="text-align:right">0.891</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Mean</strong></td><td>—</td><td style="text-align:right"><strong>0.819</strong></td><td style="text-align:right">0.799</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>Both columns are as published. The other rows are scored with the TTA and log-least-squares protocol described on their dataset pages, which the validator in this repo does not implement, so the KITTI row cannot be re-measured on the same footing; the <a href="/datasets/depth/kitti">KITTI page</a> carries numbers measured on the canonical 652-frame Eigen split instead.</p>
<p>The largest gains are on the longer-range outdoor benchmarks (KITTI, ETH3D) — exactly where a fixed 10 m ceiling hurts most — while indoor performance is retained.</p>

<p>Train YOLO26n-depth on the <a href="/datasets/depth/depth8">Depth8</a> dataset for 100 <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/epoch" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">epochs</a> at image size 640. <h3 id="fine-tuning-on-your-own-data" class="group relative">Fine-tuning on your own data<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#fine-tuning-on-your-own-data">#</a></h3>
<p>When adapting a pretrained depth model to a custom dataset, <strong>lower the learning rate and use the AdamW optimizer</strong>. The default optimizer settings are tuned for training from scratch (SGD with <code>lr0=0.01</code>); applied to an already-converged depth model they can overwrite the pretrained knowledge and degrade results — especially when fine-tuning on a single domain.</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 recipe</span></div><div class="admon-content"><div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" data-slot="tabs" class="group/tabs flex min-w-0 max-w-full gap-4 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26s-depth.pt&quot;)  # start from pretrained weights

model.train(
    data=&quot;path/to/your_dataset.yaml&quot;,
    epochs=20,
    imgsz=640,
    optimizer=&quot;AdamW&quot;,
    lr0=1e-4,  # ~100x below the from-scratch default
    warmup_bias_lr=1e-4,  # keep warmup gentle too
)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><p>Additional tips:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Augmentation is controlled by the standard args</strong> (<code>degrees</code>, <code>translate</code>, <code>scale</code>, <code>shear</code>, <code>perspective</code>, <code>flipud</code>, <code>fliplr</code>, <code>hsv_h</code>, <code>hsv_s</code>, <code>hsv_v</code>); the geometric warp and flips are applied identically to the paired depth map. To see the exact recipe used for the released YOLO26-Depth weights, inspect the <code>train_args</code> stored in the checkpoint — see <a href="/guides/yolo26-training-recipe#inspecting-yolo26-checkpoint-training-args">Inspecting YOLO26 Checkpoint Training Args</a>.</li>
<li><strong><code>mosaic</code>, <code>mixup</code>, <code>cutmix</code>, and <code>copy_paste</code> are not implemented for depth.</strong> The depth dataset loader automatically sets these probabilities to 0, so passing them has no effect. These augmentations are not supported because they combine multiple images, which would produce invalid paired depth maps.</li>
<li><strong>Any depth range works out of the box.</strong> The <code>log</code>-head models predict unbounded depth, so they adapt to short-range (macro) or long-range (outdoor/driving) data without changes. Setting <code>max_depth:</code> in your dataset YAML (in meters) bounds which GT pixels count toward validation metrics.</li>
<li><strong>Retain general performance.</strong> If you need the model to stay accurate on scenes beyond your training set, mix a small fraction (~5–10%) of diverse general-purpose images into your training data; this substantially reduces forgetting during fine-tuning.</li>
<li><strong>Train from scratch</strong> (<code>model=yolo26s-depth.yaml</code>) only if your domain is very different and you have a large dataset — there the default SGD <code>lr0=0.01</code> is appropriate, since there are no pretrained weights to preserve.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="calibrating-the-depth-scale" class="group relative">Calibrating the depth scale<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#calibrating-the-depth-scale">#</a></h3>
<p>The depth head separates <strong>shape</strong> (relative scene structure) from <strong>scale</strong> (absolute meters). If a model already produces good relative depth on your scenes but the absolute values are off for your camera, you can correct the scale in seconds with <code>model.calibrate()</code> — a closed-form fit of a two-parameter log-affine against a small labeled set, with <strong>no gradient training and no change to the network weights</strong>, so it cannot degrade the relative structure.</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-flask-conical admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M14 2v6a2 2 0 0 0 .245.96l5.51 10.08A2 2 0 0 1 18 22H6a2 2 0 0 1-1.755-2.96l5.51-10.08A2 2 0 0 0 10 8V2"></path><path d="M6.453 15h11.094"></path><path d="M8.5 2h7"></path></svg><span>Scale calibration</span></div><div class="admon-content"><div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" data-slot="tabs" class="group/tabs flex min-w-0 max-w-full gap-4 data-horizontal:flex-col my-8"><div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" role="tablist" data-slot="tabs-list" data-variant="default" class="group/tabs-list inline-flex w-fit max-w-full items-center justify-center rounded-lg p-[3px] text-muted-foreground group-data-horizontal/tabs:h-8 group-data-horizontal/tabs:justify-start group-data-horizontal/tabs:overflow-x-auto group-data-horizontal/tabs:overflow-y-hidden group-data-horizontal/tabs:overscroll-x-contain group-data-horizontal/tabs:touch-pan-x group-data-vertical/tabs:h-fit group-data-vertical/tabs:flex-col data-[variant=line]:rounded-none bg-muted"><button type="button" data-active="" data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" aria-disabled="false" tabindex="-1" role="tab" aria-selected="true" id="base-ui-_R_5p15l6anpampfivb_" data-composite-item-active="" data-slot="tabs-trigger" class="relative inline-flex h-[calc(100%-1px)] flex-1 items-center justify-center gap-1.5 rounded-md border border-transparent px-1.5 py-0.5 text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap text-foreground/60 transition-all group-data-vertical/tabs:w-full group-data-vertical/tabs:justify-start hover:text-foreground focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-[3px] focus-visible:ring-ring/50 focus-visible:outline-1 focus-visible:outline-ring disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 has-data-[icon=inline-end]:pr-1 has-data-[icon=inline-start]:pl-1 aria-disabled:pointer-events-none aria-disabled:opacity-50 dark:text-muted-foreground dark:hover:text-foreground [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 group-data-[variant=line]/tabs-list:bg-transparent group-data-[variant=line]/tabs-list:data-active:bg-transparent data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" id="base-ui-_R_6p15l6anpampfivb_" role="tabpanel" tabindex="0" data-index="-1" data-slot="tabs-content" class="min-w-0 max-w-full flex-1 text-sm outline-none mkdocs-tabs-content"><div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_1uup15l6anpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26s-depth.pt&quot;)

# Fit scale on a labeled split (~100+ images is enough), then persist it
model.calibrate(data=&quot;path/to/your_dataset.yaml&quot;)
model.save(&quot;yolo26s-depth-calibrated.pt&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><p>Calibration needs ground-truth depth to fit against, so it runs on a labeled split — it is not something that can happen at blind inference. It fits and scores on the same pixels the val metrics evaluate: GT at or beyond the dataset YAML&#x27;s <code>max_depth</code> (default 100 m) is excluded. Use it when relative depth is already good and only the scale/range is wrong; if the relative structure itself needs to change for your domain, <a href="#fine-tuning-on-your-own-data">fine-tune</a> instead.</p>
<p>Training does this for you automatically: after <code>model.train(...)</code> completes, the best and last checkpoints are calibrated on the validation set so they output metric-scaled depth out of the box.</p>
<p>The released <code>yolo26*-depth.pt</code> checkpoints ship with this calibration already baked in, fit on the pretraining validation mix. It is a single global scale across all domains, so for the most accurate absolute depth on a specific camera or scene type, run <code>model.calibrate()</code> on a small labeled split from your own data — it replaces the baked-in fit.</p>
<h3 id="dataset-format" class="group relative">Dataset format<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#dataset-format">#</a></h3>
<p>Depth estimation datasets pair each RGB image with a scaled uint16 depth PNG or floating-point NPY depth map in meters. PNG values use millimeters by default; datasets with another convention set <code>depth_scale</code> in their YAML. The loader derives the depth path by replacing the <code>images</code> component with <code>depth</code>, preferring <code>.png</code> and falling back to <code>.npy</code>.</p>
<div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_7pd55l6anpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>dataset/
├── images/
│   ├── train/
│   └── val/
└── depth/
    ├── train/
    └── val/</code></pre></div>
<p>For example, an image at <code>images/train/scene_001.jpg</code> is paired with a depth map at <code>depth/train/scene_001.png</code>. See the <a href="/datasets/depth">Depth Estimation Dataset Guide</a> for the full format specification.</p>
<h2 id="val" class="group relative">Val<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#val">#</a></h2>
<p>Validate a trained YOLO26n-depth model <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/accuracy" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">accuracy</a> on a depth estimation dataset. Pass <code>data</code> explicitly so validation uses the intended dataset YAML. # Predict with the model
results = model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;)  # predict on an image

# Access the results
for result in results:
    depth_map = result.depth.data.cpu().numpy()  # torch.Tensor -&gt; NumPy float32, shape (H, W), meters</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><p>See full <code>predict</code> mode details in the <a href="/modes/predict">Predict</a> page.</p>
<h3 id="results-output" class="group relative">Results Output<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#results-output">#</a></h3>
<p>YOLO depth estimation returns one <code>Results</code> object per image. Each result stores one dense float depth map for the full image.</p>



































<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Attribute</th><th>Type</th><th>Shape</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>result.depth</code></td><td><code>DepthMap</code></td><td><code>(H,W)</code></td><td>Dense per-pixel depth map.</td></tr><tr><td><code>result.depth.data</code></td><td><code>torch.Tensor</code></td><td><code>(H,W)</code></td><td>Depth values in meters; call <code>.cpu().numpy()</code> for NumPy.</td></tr><tr><td><code>result.boxes</code></td><td>-</td><td>-</td><td>No instance boxes.</td></tr><tr><td><code>result.masks</code></td><td>-</td><td>-</td><td>No instance masks.</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>For task-specific <code>Results</code> fields across every task, see the <a href="/modes/predict#results-by-task">Predict Results by Task</a> section.</p>
<h3 id="colorizing-the-depth-map" class="group relative">Colorizing the depth map<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#colorizing-the-depth-map">#</a></h3>
<p>The raw depth map is a single-channel float array in meters — useful for computation, but hard to read directly. To turn it into a color image, use the <code>colorize_depth</code> helper in <code>ultralytics.utils.plotting</code>, which maps the <code>(H, W)</code> depth array to a <code>(H, W, 3)</code> BGR <code>uint8</code> image (invalid pixels <code>&lt;= 0</code> are rendered black).</p>
<p><code>result.plot()</code> already blends this colorization over the input image using the defaults (<code>cmap=&quot;jet&quot;</code>, <code>mode=&quot;disparity&quot;</code>); call <code>colorize_depth</code> directly when you want a standalone colored depth image or a different colormap or normalization.</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-flask-conical admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M14 2v6a2 2 0 0 0 .245.96l5.51 10.08A2 2 0 0 1 18 22H6a2 2 0 0 1-1.755-2.96l5.51-10.08A2 2 0 0 0 10 8V2"></path><path d="M6.453 15h11.094"></path><path d="M8.5 2h7"></path></svg><span>Colorize a predicted depth map</span></div><div class="admon-content"><div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" group-data-[variant=line]/tabs-list:data-active:after:opacity-100 mkdocs-tabs-trigger">Python</button></div><div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" id="base-ui-_R_6pp5l6anpampfivb_" role="tabpanel" tabindex="0" data-index="-1" data-slot="tabs-content" class="min-w-0 max-w-full flex-1 text-sm outline-none mkdocs-tabs-content"><div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_1uupp5l6anpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>import cv2

from ultralytics import YOLO
from ultralytics.utils.plotting import colorize_depth

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n-depth.pt&quot;)
result = model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;)[0]

depth = result.depth.data.cpu().numpy()  # (H, W) float32, meters

# Colorize with near = warm and save
cv2.imwrite(&quot;depth_colored.png&quot;, colorize_depth(depth, cmap=&quot;spectral&quot;))  # (H, W, 3) BGR uint8

# Fix the range to 0-20 m so the same color means the same distance across frames
cv2.imwrite(&quot;depth_metric.png&quot;, colorize_depth(depth, vmin=0.0, vmax=20.0, cmap=&quot;inferno&quot;, mode=&quot;metric&quot;))

# Blended overlay straight from the Results object (uses cmap=&quot;jet&quot;, mode=&quot;disparity&quot;)
result.save(&quot;depth_overlay.png&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><p>Every colormap runs cool/dark → warm/bright. The <code>mode</code> decides which end is near, and <code>vmin</code>/<code>vmax</code> lock the range across frames so a color always means the same distance.</p>



































<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Argument</th><th>Value</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>cmap</code></td><td><code>jet</code> (default)</td><td>High-contrast blue → green → red, the palette <code>result.plot()</code> uses.</td></tr><tr><td><code>cmap</code></td><td><code>inferno</code></td><td>Black → purple → orange → yellow. Perceptually uniform, colorblind-friendly, readable in grayscale.</td></tr><tr><td><code>cmap</code></td><td><code>spectral</code></td><td>Red → yellow → green → blue (matplotlib <code>Spectral_r</code>).</td></tr><tr><td><code>mode</code></td><td><code>disparity</code> (default)</td><td>Normalizes inverse depth (<code>1/d</code>) between the 2nd and 98th percentiles; warm marks near and far outliers are absorbed.</td></tr><tr><td><code>mode</code></td><td><code>metric</code></td><td>Normalizes depth in meters linearly between <code>vmin</code> and <code>vmax</code>; warm marks far, so colors track true distance.</td></tr></tbody></table></div>

<p><code>nms=None</code> defaults to raw outputs for external NMS. Set <code>nms=False</code> to select an available NMS-free head; unsupported formats fall back to their native output path. The <code>nms</code> entries above identify formats that can embed NMS with <code>nms=True</code>.</p>
<p>See full <code>export</code> details in the <a href="/modes/export">Export</a> page.</p>
<h2 id="faq" class="group relative">FAQ<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#faq">#</a></h2></div><script type="application/ld+json">{"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"FAQPage","mainEntity":[{"@type":"Question","name":"How do I train a YOLO26 depth estimation model on a custom dataset?","acceptedAnswer":{"@type":"Answer","text":"Prepare paired RGB images and either 16-bit depth PNGs or floating-point NPY depth maps in meters, then create a dataset YAML pointing to your images/ directory. Higher is better. abs_rel — mean absolute relative error. Lower is better. rmse — root mean squared error in meters. Lower is better. silog — scale-invariant logarithmic error. Lower is better. Each prediction is median-aligned to its ground truth per image, each metric is finalized on that image, and those per-image results are averaged over the validation set, so every image weighs the same regardless of how many valid depth pixels it holds. Images with fewer than 10 valid ground-truth pixels are skipped and do not enter that average, since aligning the median of a handful of pixels is meaningless; non-finite predictions are instead scored at the depth bounds. <ul>
<li><strong>delta1 / delta2 / delta3</strong> — percentage of pixels where the ratio of predicted to ground-truth depth (or its inverse) is below 1.25, 1.25², and 1.25³ respectively. Higher is better.</li>
<li><strong>abs_rel</strong> — mean absolute relative error. Lower is better.</li>
<li><strong>rmse</strong> — root mean squared error in meters. Lower is better.</li>
<li><strong>silog</strong> — scale-invariant logarithmic error. Lower is better.</li>
</ul>
<p>Each prediction is median-aligned to its ground truth per image, each metric is finalized on that image, and those per-image results are averaged over the validation set, so every image weighs the same regardless of how many valid depth pixels it holds. Images with fewer than 10 valid ground-truth pixels are skipped and do not enter that average, since aligning the median of a handful of pixels is meaningless; non-finite predictions are instead scored at the depth bounds. This matches the per-sample averaging and the 10-pixel floor used by Depth Anything V2.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-is-the-depth-map-output-format"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-is-the-depth-map-output-format-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What is the depth map output format?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-is-the-depth-map-output-format-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>The depth map is stored as a <code>torch.Tensor</code> of shape <code>(H, W)</code> where each value is the predicted depth in meters (use <code>result.depth.data.cpu().numpy()</code> for a NumPy <code>float32</code> array). Access it through <code>result.depth.data</code> after running prediction. The map is aligned to the input image resolution.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-datasets-are-supported-for-depth-estimation"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-datasets-are-supported-for-depth-estimation-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What datasets are supported for depth estimation?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-datasets-are-supported-for-depth-estimation-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Ultralytics YOLO26 provides built-in dataset YAML configurations for several depth estimation datasets including NYU Depth V2, KITTI, Hypersim, SUN RGB-D, and ARKitScenes. See the <a href="/datasets/depth">Depth Estimation Dataset Guide</a> for the full list and format details.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="how-do-i-validate-a-pretrained-yolo26-depth-estimation-model"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="how-do-i-validate-a-pretrained-yolo26-depth-estimation-model-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">How do I validate a pretrained YOLO26 depth estimation model?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="how-do-i-validate-a-pretrained-yolo26-depth-estimation-model-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Validate a pretrained YOLO26 depth model by supplying the dataset YAML used for evaluation:</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" The default optimizer settings are tuned for training from scratch (SGD with \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"lr0=0.01\"}],\"); applied to an already-converged depth model they can overwrite the pretrained knowledge and degrade results — especially when fine-tuning on a single domain.\"]}]]}]\n3e:[\"$\",\"section\",\"admonition-5\",{\"className\":\"admon admon-example\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"admon-title\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L29\",null,{\"ref\":\"$undefined\",\"icon\":\"$3c:props:children:0:props:children:0:props:icon\",\"className\":\"admon-icon\",\"aria-hidden\":true}],[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Recommended fine-tuning recipe\"}],null]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"admon-content\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L72\",\"tabs-0\",{\"className\":\"my-8\",\"defaultValue\":\"tabs-0-0\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L73\",null,{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L74\",\"tabs-0-0\",{\"className\":\"mkdocs-tabs-trigger\",\"value\":\"tabs-0-0\",\"children\":\"Python\"}],[\"$\",\"$L74\",\"tabs-0-1\",{\"className\":\"mkdocs-tabs-trigger\",\"value\":\"tabs-0-1\",\"children\":\"CLI\"}]]}],[[\"$\",\"$L75\",\"tabs-0-0\",{\"className\":\"mkdocs-tabs-content\",\"value\":\"tabs-0-0\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L76\",\"markdown-0,pre-0,code-0\",{\"code\":\"from ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\nmodel = YOLO(\\\"yolo26s-depth.pt\\\")  # keep warmup gentle too\\n)\",\"language\":\"python\"}]]}],[\"$\",\"$L75\",\"tabs-0-1\",{\"className\":\"mkdocs-tabs-content\",\"value\":\"tabs-0-1\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L76\",\"markdown-0,pre-0,code-0\",{\"code\":\"yolo depth train data=path/to/your_dataset.yaml model=yolo26s-depth.pt \\\\\\n  epochs=20 imgsz=640 optimizer=AdamW lr0=1e-4 warmup_bias_lr=1e-4\",\"language\":\"bash\"}]]}]]]}]]}]]}]\n3f:[\"$\",\"$1\",\"markdown-6\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"p-0\",{\"children\":\"Additional tips:\"}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"ul\",\"ul-0\",{\"children\":[\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",\"li-0\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-0\",{\"children\":\"Augmentation is controlled by the standard args\"}],\" (\",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"degrees\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-1\",{\"children\":\"translate\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-2\",{\"children\":\"scale\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-3\",{\"children\":\"shear\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-4\",{\"children\":\"perspective\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-5\",{\"children\":\"flipud\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-6\",{\"children\":\"fliplr\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-7\",{\"children\":\"hsv_h\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-8\",{\"children\":\"hsv_s\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-9\",{\"children\":\"hsv_v\"}],\"); the geometric warp and flips are applied identically to the paired depth map. To see the exact recipe used for the released YOLO26-Depth weights, inspect the \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-10\",{\"children\":\"train_args\"}],\" stored in the checkpoint — see \",[\"$\",\"$L77\",\"a-0\",{\"href\":\"/guides/yolo26-training-recipe#inspecting-yolo26-checkpoint-training-args\",\"children\":\"Inspecting YOLO26 Checkpoint Training Args\"}],\".\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",\"li-1\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-0\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"mosaic\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-1\",{\"children\":\"mixup\"}],\", \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-2\",{\"children\":\"cutmix\"}],\", and \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-3\",{\"children\":\"copy_paste\"}],\" are not implemented for depth.\"]}],\" The depth dataset loader automatically sets these probabilities to 0, so passing them has no effect. These augmentations are not supported because they combine multiple images, which would produce invalid paired depth maps.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",\"li-2\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-0\",{\"children\":\"Any depth range works out of the box.\"}],\" The \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"log\"}],\"-head models predict unbounded depth, so they adapt to short-range (macro) or long-range (outdoor/driving) data without changes. Setting \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-1\",{\"children\":\"max_depth:\"}],\" in your dataset YAML (in meters) bounds which GT pixels count toward validation metrics.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",\"li-3\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-0\",{\"children\":\"Retain general performance.\"}],\" If you need the model to stay accurate on scenes beyond your training set, mix a small fraction (~5–10%) of diverse general-purpose images into your training data; this substantially reduces forgetting during fine-tuning.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",\"li-4\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-0\",{\"children\":\"Train from scratch\"}],\" (\",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"model=yolo26s-depth.yaml\"}],\") only if your domain is very different and you have a large dataset — there the default SGD \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-1\",{\"children\":\"lr0=0.01\"}],\" is appropriate, since there are no pretrained weights to preserve.\"]}],\"\\n\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"h3\",\"h3-0\",{\"id\":\"calibrating-the-depth-scale\",\"className\":\"group relative\",\"children\":[\"Calibrating the depth scale\",[\"$\",\"a\",null,{\"aria-label\":\"Link to this section\",\"className\":\"static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground\",\"href\":\"#calibrating-the-depth-scale\",\"children\":\"#\"}]]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",\"p-1\",{\"children\":[\"The depth head separates \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-0\",{\"children\":\"shape\"}],\" (relative scene structure) from \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-1\",{\"children\":\"scale\"}],\" (absolute meters). If a model already produces good relative depth on your scenes but the absolute values are off for your camera, you can correct the scale in seconds with \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"model.calibrate()\"}],\" — a closed-form fit of a two-parameter log-affine against a small labeled set, with \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"strong-2\",{\"children\":\"no gradient training and no change to the network weights\"}],\", so it cannot degrade the relative structure.\"]}]]}]\n40:[\"$\",\"section\",\"admonition-7\",{\"className\":\"admon admon-example\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"admon-title\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L29\",null,{\"ref\":\"$undefined\",\"icon\":\"$3c:props:children:0:props:children:0:props:icon\",\"className\":\"admon-icon\",\"aria-hidden\":true}],[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"Scale calibration\"}],null]}],[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"className\":\"admon-content\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L72\",\"tabs-0\",{\"className\":\"my-8\",\"defaultValue\":\"tabs-0-0\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L73\",null,{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L74\",\"tabs-0-0\",{\"className\":\"mkdocs-tabs-trigger\",\"value\":\"tabs-0-0\",\"children\":\"Python\"}]]}],[[\"$\",\"$L75\",\"tabs-0-0\",{\"className\":\"mkdocs-tabs-content\",\"value\":\"tabs-0-0\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L76\",\"markdown-0,pre-0,code-0\",{\"code\":\"from ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\nmodel = YOLO(\\\"yolo26s-depth.pt\\\")\\n\\n# Fit scale on a labeled split (~100+ images is enough), then persist it\\nmodel.calibrate(data=\\\"path/to/your_dataset.yaml\\\")\\nmodel.save(\\\"yolo26s-depth-calibrated.pt\\\")\",\"language\":\"python\"}]]}]]]}]]}]]}]\n41:[\"$\",\"$1\",\"markdown-8\",{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"p\",\"p-0\",{\"children\":[\"Calibration needs ground-truth depth to fit against, so it runs on a labeled split — it is not something that can happen at blind inference. It fits and scores on the same pixels the val metrics evaluate: GT at or beyond the dataset YAML's \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"max_depth\"}],\" (default 100 m) is excluded. Use it when relative depth is already good and only the scale/range is wrong; if the relative structure itself needs to change for your domain, \",[\"$\",\"a\",\"a-0\",{\"className\":\"$undefined\",\"href\":\"#fine-tuning-on-your-own-data\",\"children\":\"fine-tune\"}],\" instead.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",\"p-1\",{\"children\":[\"Training does this for you automatically: after \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"model.train(...)\"}],\" completes, the best and last checkpoints are calibrated on the validation set so they output metric-scaled depth out of the box.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",\"p-2\",{\"children\":[\"The released \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"yolo26*-depth.pt\"}],\" checkpoints ship with this calibration already baked in, fit on the pretraining validation mix. It is a single global scale across all domains, so for the most accurate absolute depth on a specific camera or scene type, run \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-1\",{\"children\":\"model.calibrate()\"}],\" on a small labeled split from your own data — it replaces the baked-in fit.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"h3\",\"h3-0\",{\"id\":\"dataset-format\",\"className\":\"group relative\",\"children\":[\"Dataset format\",[\"$\",\"a\",null,{\"aria-label\":\"Link to this section\",\"className\":\"static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground\",\"href\":\"#dataset-format\",\"children\":\"#\"}]]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",\"p-3\",{\"children\":[\"Depth estimation datasets pair each RGB image with a scaled uint16 depth PNG or floating-point NPY depth map in meters. PNG values use millimeters by default; datasets with another convention set \",[\"$\",\"code\",\"code-0\",{\"children\":\"depth_scale\"}],\" in their YAML. do I train a YOLO26 depth estimation model on a custom dataset?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"Prepare paired RGB images and either 16-bit depth PNGs or floating-point NPY depth maps in meters, then create a dataset YAML pointing to your images/ directory. The loader finds depth files automatically by replacing images with depth in the path, preferring .png and falling back to .npy. Start from pretrained weights and use a low learning rate with AdamW so the fine-tune retains what the model already knows (see Fine-tuning on your own data for why): Check the Configuration page for more available arguments.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What metrics does YOLO26 depth estimation report?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"Depth estimation validation reports the metric set used by Depth Anything and related monocular-depth work: delta1 / delta2 / delta3 — percentage of pixels where the ratio of predicted to ground-truth depth (or its inverse) is below 1.25, 1.25², and 1.25³ respectively. Higher is better. abs_rel — mean absolute relative error. Lower is better. rmse — root mean squared error in meters. Lower is better. silog — scale-invariant logarithmic error. Lower is better. Each prediction is median-aligned to its ground truth per image, each metric is finalized on that image, and those per-image results are averaged over the validation set, so every image weighs the same regardless of how many valid depth pixels it holds. Images with fewer than 10 valid ground-truth pixels are skipped and do not enter that average, since aligning the median of a handful of pixels is meaningless; non-finite predictions are instead scored at the depth bounds. This matches the per-sample averaging and the 10-pixel floor used by Depth Anything V2.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What is the depth map output format?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"The depth map is stored as a torch.Tensor of shape (H, W) where each value is the predicted depth in meters (use result.depth.data.cpu().numpy() for a NumPy float32 array). Access it through result.depth.data after running prediction. The map is aligned to the input image resolution.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"What datasets are supported for depth estimation?\",\"acceptedAnswer\":{\"@type\":\"Answer\",\"text\":\"Ultralytics YOLO26 provides built-in dataset YAML configurations for several depth estimation datasets including NYU Depth V2, KITTI, Hypersim, SUN RGB-D, and ARKitScenes. See the Depth Estimation Dataset Guide for the full list and format details.\"}},{\"@type\":\"Question\",\"name\":\"How do I validate a pretrained YOLO26 depth estimation
